Exception specifications, recovery models and explicit failure limits support bounded mechanisms. They do not measure the frequency or reversibility of arbitrary external effects.</p></dd></dl></dd><dt>Decision or consumer</dt><dd><p>Recovery coordinator and owner of the external action.</p></dd><dt>Actor and authority boundary</dt><dd><p>Timeout does not authorise a conflicting second action or unilateral reversal.</p></dd><dt>Communication or interaction requirement</dt><dd><p>Communicate “response absent/effect unknown” rather than “did not happen” when that is all the evidence supports.</p></dd><dt>Observable postcondition</dt><dd><p>The committed-before-timeout case cannot silently produce an unjustified duplicate effect.</p></dd><dt>Known failure modes</dt><dd><ol><li><p>Retry or compensation repeats or reverses an action on a false assumption of failure.</p></li><li><p>Uncertainty may require human adjudication or long waits; querying can itself fail.</p></li></ol></dd><dt>Important criticisms</dt><dd><dl><dt>Adverse mechanism or cost</dt><dd><p>Uncertainty may require human adjudication or long waits; querying can itself fail.</p></dd><dt>Criticism ids</dt><dd><ol><li><p>EWM-C010</p></li></ol></dd><dt>Tension ids</dt><dd><ol><li><p>EWM-T004</p></li></ol></dd><dt>Critical source ids</dt><dd><ol><li><p>EWM-S029</p></li></ol></dd><dt>Adjudication</dt><dd><p>Strongly retain epistemic distinction; no blanket rule against retries.</p></dd></dl></dd><dt>How the property evolved</dt><dd><p>PRESERVED/CLARIFIED: loss detection and effect knowledge are kept separate across long-lived and durable execution.</p></dd><dt>Mature or evolved form</dt><dd><p>Treat a timed-out action’s effect as unknown until the relevant protocol, idempotency boundary or reconciliation establishes what happened.
